# ROADMAP — linux-postinstall redesign

> INTERNAL EXECUTION PLAN — development only.
> This file is gitignored and MUST NOT be committed.

This roadmap governs the clean rewrite of the current prototype (`setup.sh` + `scripts/*`)
into a structured, distro-family multi-backend workstation bootstrap tool.

---

## Legend

Task tags (appended to each task line):

| Tag | Meaning |
|-----|---------|
| `[SEQ]`   | Must run strictly after its listed predecessor (dependent). |
| `[PAR]`   | Independent of siblings; safe to run in parallel with other `[PAR]` tasks. |
| `[MOCK]`  | Fully testable with mocks / fixtures, no root, no real system needed. |
| `[REAL]`  | Requires or strongly benefits from a real Fedora/Debian/Arch system. |
| `[DESTROY]`| Destructive or modifies system/user state; special care + explicit confirmation required. |

Sequencing rule: a task may only start when every task in its `Depends:` line is done, unless
marked `[PAR]` (then it is independent of the other tasks in the same group).

---

| P7.5 VS Code module | **DONE** · **COMMITTED** `b5e50ce` | rev `ses_f130947f7ffeq9QWe37lcpbPSj` (R1 **REVISE** B1/B2 + 8 NB → R2 **REVISE** B-A + 8 NB → R3 **PASS**, no blocking survived; retry after a transport failure that returned an empty result). `lib/runner.sh` pre-batch **PREREQ** stage (6 stages now): per module in resolved order `prerepo.sh` (if present) sourced in a subshell, `prerepo()` called ONCE, ALWAYS (never state-skipped — the P3 `add_repo` primitives are `--if-not-exists`, so it self-heals a deleted repo file) and BEFORE both batches; `state_init` stays after the batches. Fail-fast whatever the risk (rc1, no batch/hooks/summary), deliberately NOT the safe-continue policy a missing/failing `run()` gets, including a `prerepo.sh` that never defines `prerepo()` — prereq is detected before anything is installed, so stopping costs no work and no state, while continuing would install that module's packages anyway. Dry-run DOES call prerepo (it renders its own steps) so a dry run shows repo work where a real run does it. Both hook subshells `export FS_MODULE_FAMILY` and call `module_load <dir> strict || exit 1` BEFORE sourcing (B1; the bare `if ! ( … )` condition suspends errexit, hence the explicit `|| exit 1`). `lib/modules.sh`: `module_has_prerepo`, plus the flatpak-alternative pair `MODULE_FLATPAK_ALT_ID` + `MODULE_FLATPAK_ALT_SEAM` (an env-var name) resolved by `module_flatpak_alt` (truthy `1|true|yes|on`, case-insensitive), all-or-nothing validation (id without seam, or a seam that is not an env-var name, fails), never an `if` in a list file. Truthy seam → the id goes to the FLATPAK namespace and the module contributes NOTHING to the SYSTEM namespace: the SYSTEM list files are not even read, so a stale native id there can never reach a batch. Scoped to SYSTEM — the module's own `flatpaks.list` is still read and is additive (NB8 from R2: the original `continue` also skipped it). Exclusivity is a **within-one-run** guarantee only: the state registry gates hooks, not planning or batches, so flipping the seam after a native install keeps both on disk (documented in AGENTS §12 + module.sh; switching is a manual uninstall). `modules/vscode/` (module.sh RISK=medium DEFAULT=on, no hooks.sh — an empty one would fail the module, and a `run()` step is the wrong place for a repository; `packages.{rpm,deb}.list` = `code`; `packages.arch.list` comment-only): `prerepo.sh` is the first module to pin a third-party repository key — the key is **fetched and verified at run time, never vendored**, so a first install needs network (accepted trade-off; the tool has no offline/cache story to borrow). Trust: the full 40-hex **PRIMARY**-key fingerprint from `gpg --show-keys --with-colons` must equal the pin in the new `config/vscode-gpg.fingerprint`, else no import, no repo file, rc1; a key file holding **more than one key** is refused outright, and only the first `fpr:` after the only `pub:` is captured (B-A: gpg emits `pub,fpr,uid,sub,fpr`, so a naive "last fpr wins" read pins the SUBKEY — a strictly weaker anchor — and would have led a maintainer to pin a subkey at the next rotation, the normal modern layout). rpm imports into the rpmdb and the repo file relies on it (`gpgcheck=1`, no `gpgkey=`); deb dearmors to a TOOL-OWNED keyring pinned with `signed-by=` (never overwrites a distro-shipped one); `pkg_add_repo`'s rc is untrustworthy for a failed write so the postcondition (file exists, non-empty) is checked after the call, and `pkg_update_metadata` runs explicitly because `_deb_repos_changed` cannot cross the subshell. Owner decisions: **no native Arch/AUR path** (`vscode` warns + no-op on arch; `FS_VSCODE_FLATPAK=1`) — the task text's AUR is deviated from deliberately, since `visual-studio-code-bin` needs an AUR helper and no official Arch package exists; the task text's `vscode.repo` is also stale (both `yumrepos/code.repo` and `yumrepos/vscode.repo` 404 today; the vendor serves a repo DIRECTORY whose `config.repo` baseurl is the directory itself, written by the P3 layer). Seams: `FS_VSCODE_FLATPAK`, `FS_VSCODE_KEY_FILE`, `FS_VSCODE_KEYRING`; `FS_MODULE_FAMILY` is not a seam — the runner exports it into both hook subshells so a hook never re-detects the distro. `tests/fixtures/mod_vscode.sh` (new, **160 asserts**): metadata/alt-pair validity, seam truthiness + reset, dry ordering (prereq render strictly before both batches, nothing executed), fingerprint mismatch (no import, no repo file), match on rpm (import + repo file + `makecache`, no keyring) and deb (dearmor + `signed-by=` line, no rpmdb import), the two-module `pair`-profile exclusivity cells (seam truthy after a LATER module ⇒ no native repo work at all; seam unset ⇒ repo work still happens — so the cell above is not green because prerepo stopped running), the primary-fingerprint-vs-subkey cells (a key with a signing subkey is accepted on its primary; a key with a WRONG primary whose subkey equals the pin is still refused), the 2-key bundle cells (rpm + deb, refused, nothing imported/dearmored/repo-added), idempotent re-run, missing/malformed pin, missing `gpg`/`curl`, dearmor-writes-nothing, fetch failure, arch + flatpak no-ops, absent family. `tests/fixtures/runner.sh` **84→137**: prereq order relative to BOTH batches, always-run, fail-fast on a LOW-risk module, `prerepo.sh` with no `prerepo()`, dry-run rendering before the batch, `FS_MODULE_FAMILY` exported to a child process, hook-subshell isolation — each hook records the `MODULE_ID` it can see and a module sorting after the prerepo module makes the stale-metadata case reachable through the runner alone (B1), plus the alt-pair cell (alternative + own flatpak present, stale native id absent, no system batch). `lib/modules.sh` docs: "six"→EIGHT contract globals; a missing `prerepo()` documents a whole-run stop, not a per-module failure. `AGENTS.md` §1 (P7.1–P7.4 = apps/media/dev/containers; `setup verify` still not implemented, P9.2; `lib/depgraph.sh` is P4.4 — B2 from R1), §2 runner/modules rows, §3 P7.5 seams, §12 (alt-pair exclusivity + its within-one-run scope; single-key bundle refusal; the deliberate offline trade-off). Evidence: mod_vscode **160/0** rc0, runner **137/0** rc0, smoke **125/0** rc0, all **34** fixture suites rc0 (**2310** asserts), `bash -n` clean on every changed/new shell file, stdout md5 byte-identical ×3 each with empty stderr, green under `env -i` and from a foreign CWD, modes 644. Gate-proves (mutation site → effect, all byte-restored, rc1): remove `module_load … || exit 1` from both hook subshells (B1 revert) → mod_vscode **4 FAIL** + runner **2 FAIL**, with `repodata/repomd.xml.key`, `rpm --import`, `install -m 0644` and `dnf5 makecache` all reappearing = both paths side by side; drop the `-z "$fp"` half of the fingerprint guard (B-A revert) → mod_vscode **7 FAIL**; delete the `keys != 1` bundle refusal → mod_vscode **7 FAIL**; restore the alt-skip `continue` → runner **13 FAIL** incl. the alt cell; move PREREQ after the batches → runner **5 FAIL** incl. `a hook ran in the wrong order relative to the batches` + mod_vscode **2 FAIL**. Everything the container run would have covered is proven hermetically instead (real `prerepo.sh` + real P3 beans with stand-in binaries, `FS_EUID=0`, `FS_REPOS_DIR`/`FS_SOURCES_DIR` redirected, exact repo-file/sources-line/`gpgcheck` assertions, ordering, idempotent re-run, every fail-closed input). | P7.6 Chrome module | **DONE** · **COMMITTED** `efa44ad` | rev `ses_f12bcfd27ffew3DPMWqL6vUD8O` (R1 **REVISE** 1 BLOCKING + 14 NB → fixes → delta re-review in the SAME session **PASS**, 0 blocking). **R1-B1 (blocking, the one that mattered):** the install result was read from `pkg_install_local`'s rc, which CANNOT report failure — rpm/deb call `run_sudo "install local package" -- …` with **no `--stop`**, and `run_cmd`'s keep-going default logs `command failed (rc=N)` and returns 0. So a refused install reported `1 ok`, exited 0, and `lib/runner.sh` marked the module **done**, skipping Chrome on every later run (reviewer reproduced it end to end through `./setup install`). Fixed module-locally with the postcondition rule already recorded for `pkg_add_repo`: after the call, `pkg_query_installed google-chrome-stable` must be rc0, else io_error + cleanup + rc1. **No P3 change** — the `run_sudo … --stop` gap stays a separate owner ticket (§13), and the mock backend is deliberately NOT taught to record a local install because `mock_install_local` receives only a file path and no package name (inventing a filename→package convention is a P3 design change), so the fixture drives the install-happy path through the **real** rpm/deb seams with stand-ins coupled to each other (fake installer records the package; fake `rpm -q`/`dpkg-query` answer from that same file). Honest limit, documented in the header + §12: the postcondition asks "is it installed", not "did my command succeed", so a host that already has Chrome passes even if this run's install failed — a version bump that silently did not happen is the one case it cannot detect; and forcing `FS_PKG_BACKEND=mock` for a real run makes the module fail, which is the honest outcome. `modules/chrome/` (id=chrome, TITLE="Google Chrome", RISK=medium, DEFAULT=on) is **hooks-only** — no list file at all, so it contributes to NEITHER batch namespace and `module_validate`'s family gate passes on absence (a hooks-only module is a distinct case from P7.5's vscode-on-Arch, which still needs a comment-only family list). Nothing about the version is pinned in-repo: the native path resolves an architecture-pinned `(URL, SHA-256)` pair at RUN TIME from the vendor's own index (deb `Packages` Filename+SHA256; rpm `repodata/primary.xml.gz` checksum+location), both fields from the SAME stanza, then verifies the digest before the existing `pkg_install_local` seam. Two REAL bugs found and fixed while building it, both pinned: (1) bash `[[ "$x" == "$pat" ]]` is a FULL-STRING glob match, not a substring test, so the rpm `<name>` comparison never matched and the whole rpm path was dead; (2) the rpm `<location href>` is repo-relative, so the URL must carry the per-arch repo dir (`…/rpm/stable/x86_64/<file>.rpm`) or it 404s. Both indexes carry decoys (`google-chrome-repo`, `-beta`, `-canary`, `-unstable`) with their own digests, so selection is by EXACT package name. Arch warns + no-ops (no official package; the AUR needs a helper this tool does not manage), and the P7.5 alternative pair is reused unchanged (`FS_CHROME_FLATPAK=1` → `com.google.Chrome`, exclusive, never additive). Dry-run still cannot EXECUTE `pkg_install_local` — the P3 seam opens with `[[ ! -f "$file" ]]` and a zero-write dry run has no file — so it renders both wget steps (metadata URL statically derivable; bundle URL only exists after resolving the index, so it carries a literal `RESOLVED-AT-RUN-TIME` marker) and REPORTS the install step in an info line; the P3 precondition was NOT weakened for everyone else. **Trust boundary, stated so it is not misread:** the SHA-256 check is a corruption/tamper check, **NOT signature verification** — the index is trusted purely over HTTPS to `dl.google.com`, no vendor signature or pinned key is verified, and since index and bundle share that one channel the digest does not compensate for it either (it buys corruption detection and a divergent mirror/CDN edge, nothing against a compromised vendor host or a CA). Deliberately weaker than P7.5's pinned repository key because Chrome's stable channel moves every few weeks. All 14 R1 non-blocking findings fixed: INT/TERM trap on the staging dir + corrected cleanup claim; bounded fetches (`--timeout=300 --tries=2`, https-by-construction instead of wget's non-portable `--https-only`); fail-closed `module_flatpak_alt` resolution; `$(pkg_backend)` in the dry-run info line; and five cell families the reviewer had proven unpinned by mutation — rpm hostile `<location href>`, wrong-length-but-hex digest, an index whose LAST MEANINGFUL line is unterminated, substring-vs-full-string matching (target `<name>` indented differently from the decoys), and a dry-run cell on the REAL deb backend. Also fixed: the vacuous `fx_empty` OPS assert (PATH pointed at a `full` dir that was never created), the missing seam vars in the fixture's unset list, a dead `$IDX` argument, and `ch_noinstall` — which had been vacuous under the mock backend — now also asserting nothing ended up installed. Reviewer post-PASS items folded: an `io_error` on the (currently unreachable) alt-resolution failure, and the mock-backend consequence of the postcondition documented in the header. `tests/fixtures/mod_chrome.sh` (new, **236 asserts**): metadata/family-gate/alt-pair pins, arch mapping and URL tables, both parsers incl. decoys, hostile paths, hostile hrefs, missing fields, wrong-length digests, malformed/not-gzip indexes, unterminated last lines, missing tools, index-fetch/download failures, digest mismatch (never reaches the package manager), staging cleanup on every path, dry-run exact lines + order + write/network purity (mock AND real deb backend), verify cells, Flatpak exclusivity through the real runner for rpm/deb/arch, the real-refused-install cell through `./setup install` (rc1 + `module failed: chrome` + no registry mark + no residue), and the pre-seeded "already installed" cell that pins the postcondition's honest semantic. Evidence: `bash -n` clean on all four files; mod_chrome **236/0** rc0, stdout md5 `d86b0a43e4a6b832fa1cb8e06a8a3689` byte-identical ×3, 0 B stderr, green under `env -i`, from a foreign CWD, and with a hostile ambient env; **34 fixture suites (33 executable + `lib.sh` helper) all rc0, 2421 asserts, 0 failed** WITH the `/tmp/opencode/ubin` rpm shim on PATH (planner 25/0; WITHOUT the shim planner is rc1 22/3 — pre-existing, host has no rpm, P6.4-ticketed, explicitly NOT a P7.6 result); smoke **125/0** rc0; zero in-body comments in `hooks.sh`; no bash-4.3 hazards; modes 644. Mutation probes, all byte-restored, control 236/0: remove the postcondition → **229/5** (incl. `chrome was marked done after a failed install`); drop the rpm href validation → **226/8**; weaken the digest regex → **230/4**; drop the no-trailing-newline guard → **232/4**; full-string `<name>` compare with the 2-space indent → **232/2** and with no indent → **210/24**; dry-run calls `pkg_install_local` → **230/4**; decouple the query stand-ins from the installer → red; drop the per-cell installed-set reset → 12 failures. Live vendor re-verified 2026-09-29 (read-only): both index URLs and all four arch-resolved bundle URLs HTTP 200; the module's own parsers against the LIVE indexes return `google-chrome-stable_154.0.8037.57-1_amd64.deb` + `66c0645f…` and `google-chrome-stable-154.0.8037.57-1.x86_64.rpm` + `a7a5a14c…`; index `Size:` still 142114088; the arch-LESS rpm URL confirmed **404** (bug 2 against the live vendor). **`[REAL]` DEFERRED, NOT DONE — do not claim it:** the task's real mutating install and an installed `.desktop` were **not performed**; this host has no `podman`/`docker` and `unshare -Ur` fails with `write failed /proc/self/uid_map: Operation not permitted`, and a real install is out of bounds under §9. Same gap, same wording as P7.5, recorded in AGENTS §1. The real *network* path WAS exercised end to end read-only (index resolved → 142,114,088-byte bundle downloaded → digest matched → `pkg_install_local` reached with nothing installed); everything else is proven hermetically. Carried: the P3 `run_sudo "install local package" -- …` missing-`--stop` gap (owner ticket, deliberately out of scope); bash-4.3 container gate still not runnable on this host (static audit only, same as P7.5); `chrome`, like `apps`/`media`/`dev`/`containers`/`vscode`, is wired into NO profile (P7 owner decision) — run it with `./setup install --yes chrome`, or `FS_CHROME_FLATPAK=1` for the Flatpak build; the arch no-op still marks the module done, same as `vscode`. | P8.1 DNS module (NetworkManager) | **DONE** · **COMMITTED** `7544c38` | OWNER-APPROVED at the P8.1 gate, implemented and verified in one session **WITHOUT a fresh-context Senior Review** — deliberately deviated from the §5 workflow under explicit owner instruction ("Approved. Proceed with closing P8.1"), so this row records **no `ses_…` reviewer id and no R1/R2 rounds**. Treat the absence as a known process gap, not as a claim of review: **P8.1 has NOT had a Senior Reviewer PASS**, unlike P0–P7. `modules/dns/` (id=dns, TITLE="DNS servers (NetworkManager)", DESCRIPTION="Set NetworkManager DNS on the active connection (opt-in)", **RISK=destructive**, **DEFAULT=off**, hooks-only — no `packages.*.list`/`flatpaks.list` at all, so it contributes to NEITHER batch namespace and `module_validate`'s family gate passes on the absence of all three list kinds (the P7.6 chrome precedent, and the reason adding a native list file later would break the below exclusivity story). Deliberately wired into NO profile: the P4.7 high-risk opt-in is the only route, run explicitly with `./setup install --yes dns`. `hooks.sh` run()/verify(): **(a) the "high risk" confirmation is the PRE-EXISTING P4.7 mechanism** — `MODULE_RISK=destructive` makes `lib/ui.sh` print the `[high-risk]` row, never digit/`a`-toggle it, and require the opt-in prompt; the runner's destructive-failure policy then stops the whole run on a hook failure. No new gating code was written, because none was needed (P8.3 hardens this surface; see below). **(b) `nmcli`-native, never `resolv.conf`/`chattr`** — this replaces the prototype `scripts/add_google_dns.sh`, which deleted `/etc/resolv.conf`, rewrote it, applied `chattr +i` (making the file immutable so the user could not undo it) and blindly restarted NetworkManager; the module instead edits the connection PROFILE through nmcli, the supported reversible way, and the fixture pins that `resolv.conf`/`chattr` appear only in comments. **(c) connection selection, a real design decision worth recording:** `FS_DNS_CONNECTION` names it explicitly (no probe, so it renders exactly in dry-run); otherwise the active connection on the device carrying the DEFAULT ROUTE (`ip -o route show default` → `dev`, matched against `nmcli -t -f NAME,DEVICE,STATE connection show --active`). The loopback connection NM also reports as activated is NEVER selected, and 2+ remaining active connections is AMBIGUOUS → io_error + rc1. This came from a real bug found against the live host: NM reports BOTH `Home:wlp0s20f3:activated` AND `lo:lo:activated`, so the original "exactly one active connection" rule failed closed on a perfectly ordinary single-NIC host — the fix is the default-route device, because that is the connection whose DNS actually matters, with a single-non-loopback fallback when `ip` is absent. **(d) P2.5 "registry of changed connections"** → a NEW third registry in `lib/state.sh`: `notes/<key>` holding ONE opaque single-line VALUE under a validated key (`state_note` / `state_note_get` / `state_note_remove`), deliberately NOT merged with the file-copy `backups/registry` because the shapes differ; a value may not contain a newline or CR so a record can never be mistaken for several lines, and field encoding inside a value belongs to the caller. Atomic temp+rename, `_state_chain_ok`/`_state_subdir_ok` confinement and the symlink/non-regular-file refusals, identical to the existing registries. The dns module stores `name|prior_ignore|prior_dns` BEFORE the mutation, so a crash, a failed postcondition or a wrong choice always leaves a revert path. `state_init` now also creates `notes/`. **(e) postcondition rule (the P7.6 rule again, load-bearing a second time):** `run_sudo`'s rc is not trusted on its own, so after `nmcli connection modify` BOTH properties are read back and compared; a mismatch is io_error + rc1 and **the record is KEPT** (the module is NOT marked done, so a later run retries). `--revert` is a documented seam (`FS_DNS_REVERT=1`) that restores the exact recorded pair, verifies it by readback, and only then removes the record. A revert whose resolution check FAILS is io_error + rc1 and **keeps** the record: a broken resolver after "your settings are back" is exactly the state the user must be able to retry out of, so the only copy of the prior values is never deleted on a bad outcome. Reachability caveat, documented in the hook header: the runner's module registry gates HOOKS only, so an already-applied `dns` prints "already completed" and its revert needs the mark dropped first — the apply path prints both commands verbatim, and reverting an unmarked module (or a dry run) works directly. **(f) reactivation honesty, a third instance of the same untrustworthy-rc theme:** `nmcli connection up` is best-effort, but the label carries `--stop` **WITHOUT** `[DESTROY]` — `--stop` is what makes `run_cmd` propagate the failure so the hook can warn with the manual command, while the `[DESTROY]` tag would claim "abort the run" (a lie here); with neither, the keep-going default would return 0 for a failed nmcli and the warning could never fire. Name resolution is then checked with `getent hosts` (3 attempts, 1s apart, `FS_DNS_RESOLVE_ATTEMPTS` clamped 1–5) so a still-settling network is not reported broken; a MISSING getent is a warning (the change is already applied, so verification is skipped) while resolution that still fails is io_error + rc1 with the revert printed. Graceful skips (io_info, rc0) for no `nmcli`, NM not running, and no active connection; every probed value is shape-validated (a boolean must be yes/no/empty, every server a dotted quad) and anything unexpected fails closed BEFORE any write. Dry-run probes nothing: with the seam set every command renders exactly, without it the connection name only exists at run time so the plan carries the literal RESOLVED-AT-RUN-TIME marker (the P7.6 chrome convention) and the resolution check is REPORTED in an info line, not executed; no record is written. Seams: `FS_DNS_CONNECTION`, `FS_DNS_SERVERS` (default `8.8.8.8 8.8.4.4`, space-separated in, comma-normalized for nmcli), `FS_DNS_REVERT`, `FS_DNS_REACTIVATE`, `FS_DNS_CHECK_HOST`, `FS_DNS_RESOLVE_ATTEMPTS` — all added to the unset lists in `tests/fixtures/lib.sh` and `tests/smoke.sh`. `tests/fixtures/mod_dns.sh` (new, **150 asserts**, real repo modules/profiles via `./setup install`, modes 644): metadata row + "not in any profile" for all four; three dry-run cells pinning the exact `# would run:` lines **including the `%q` comma escaping** (`8.8.8.8\,8.8.4.4`) via a `grep -F` helper, zero fake invocations, and no state dir (the fake NM state compared byte-for-byte against a pristine copy); the marker cell; a custom-servers + `FS_DNS_REACTIVATE=0` cell; every skip path (non-NM, NM-down, no active connection, loopback-only); the ambiguity fail-closed cell; default-route-beats-the-other-connection (asserts the non-default profile is never touched); a full apply-ORDER cell pinning detect → read prior ×2 → modify → read back ×2 → up → getent; idempotent re-apply (no modify, and NO record written for a change never made); revert restoring non-empty prior values exactly; revert-with-no-record no-op; revert-target-mismatch fail-closed; a modify that does not stick (postcondition rc1, no mark, record KEPT); broken resolution (rc1 + revert printed, change stays applied); the settling-network retry cell asserting exactly 3 attempts; missing-getent warn; failed-reactivation warn-and-continue; the new revert-with-broken-resolution cell asserting the record survives and "revert complete" is NOT printed; invalid-seam cells (bad server list, bad connection name) proving nothing was even probed; an unreadable-probe fail-closed cell; the resolv.conf/chattr comment-only scan; all four verify() paths; and state-note confinement cells (single-line enforcement, uninitialized refusal). **One real host mutation happened during verification and is recorded rather than hidden:** an early fixture revision built its "PATH without getent" by symlinking every real binary and then failed to overwrite the `nmcli` symlink (`cp` through a symlink → `Permission denied`), so a non-dry cell ran the REAL nmcli and applied 8.8.8.8 to this machine's live `Home` profile — NM's polkit lets the session user rewrite their own profile, and the fixture sets `FS_EUID=0` so no sudo barrier applied. Root-caused from the NM audit log (`connection-update … args="connection.timestamp,ipv4.dns,ipv4.ignore-auto-dns" uid=1000`), **restored** (`nmcli connection modify Home ipv4.ignore-auto-dns no ipv4.dns ""`, readback `no`/empty, resolution still working; `sudo` needs a password here, and the restore succeeded without it for the same polkit reason). Hardened after it: `build_farm`/`install_fake` never follow a symlink (`cp --remove-destination` after `rm -f`), and a NEW `guard_fake_nmcli` asserts from the same PATH the child will see that nmcli is the fake REGULAR file (`! -L` + `cmp` against the fixture's own copy) in every non-dry cell — plus a self-test that the guard is **not vacuous** (a deliberately-leaky farm must be rejected). Re-verified after hardening: 3×150/0 with the live NM profile byte-identical before/after. A missing /etc/locale.conf is NOT an error: there is nothing to preserve and the revert record still holds the prior LANG. The fixture asserts the backup file exists, holds the pre-change bytes, is timestamped+PID-named, and is REGISTERED — and that a second run produces a DIFFERENT name, so run N cannot clobber run N-1. **(d) revert through the P8.1 state notes registry:** the prior LANG is recorded via `state_note` BEFORE the write; `FS_LOCALE_REVERT=1` restores the exact recorded value through the same localectl seam, reads it back, and only then removes the record. | P8.3 Risk gating hardening (shared with P4.7) | **DONE** · **COMMITTED** `05ed1b5` | IMPLEMENTED AND VERIFIED WITHOUT a fresh-context Senior Review — a THIRD consecutive process gap (after P8.1 and P8.2), so this row records **no `ses_…` reviewer id and no R1/R2 rounds**; treat the absence as a known gap, not as a claim of review. Scope: hardening the P4.7 surface the P8.1/P8.2 ledger rows both deferred to this task, and it found **two real defects plus one non-defect that only looked like one**. **(1) The `[high-risk]` red flag had NEVER rendered — a real, long-standing dead gate.** `lib/ui.sh` gated its red with `[[ -t 1 && -z "${FS_NO_COLOR:-}" ]]`, but `lib/io.sh:10` unconditionally normalizes `FS_NO_COLOR` to the STRING `"0"`, so `-z "0"` is false and the escape was never printed on any TTY. The P8.1/P8.2 ledger rows both record the `[high-risk]` row as a working risk signal; it was text-only. Both helpers now compare NUMERICALLY (`(( ${FS_NO_COLOR:-0} == 0 )) && [[ -t 1 ]]`), the TTY gate is kept (so piped output stays byte-stable and the existing anchored greps in `ui.sh`/`install.sh` keep matching), and the stale header comment that documented the never-true "FS_NO_COLOR is unset" condition is corrected in both files. The generalisable rule is written into `lib/io.sh`'s header: FS_NO_COLOR is normalized at source time, so EVERY color gate must compare numerically — one `-z` and the flag is dead. **(2) A single `MODULE_DEPENDS` line could install a destructive module with no consent at all — the significant find, and a real hole, not a hardening nicety.** `lib/bootstrap.sh`'s pre-seed risk filter walks only the profile's **curated** id list, and `lib/profiles.sh`'s `profile_resolve` then BFS-closes that set over `MODULE_DEPENDS` with no risk awareness; the filter therefore has no teeth over the closure, exactly like the P4.6 note that the state registry gates hooks but not planning. Reproduced live: appending `MODULE_DEPENDS="locale"` to the safe `core` module made `./setup install --dry-run --yes --profile minimal` render and run `sudo localectl set-locale LANG=en_US.UTF-8` — under `--yes`, with no opt-in row, no confirmation, and `locale` absent from every profile. Fix (`lib/runner.sh`): the runner now computes the **curated** set (the module args bootstrap passed, or — when it passed none — the profile FILE's own ids via `profile_load`) and REFUSES rc1, before any prerepo/batch/hook, on any high/destructive module present in the resolved set but not the curated set, naming the dependency-pull as the cause. The gate sits after the plan loop because `module_validate` is what classifies risk and planning is pure metadata reading (no writes, no state, no sudo), so rc1 there is as side-effect-free as a resolve error. Deliberately NOT extended to the curated case: a high-risk module a profile genuinely names is dropped from the checklist and the run continues rc0, which is the reviewed P4.7 contract that `install.sh` still pins — the fixture asserts both halves. **(3) The `!!` dry-run alert, the task's explicit "dry-run shows bold warning lines".** New `io_alert` in `lib/io.sh`: stdout (not the level's stderr) and un-timestamped, because it annotates a rendered plan and must interleave with the plan lines in order; styled bold+red on a TTY, byte-identical `!! <msg>` when piped so no fixture assertion ever has to know about ANSI; audit-logged through `_io_logline` under the same prefix. `lib/runner.sh` emits one per high/destructive module in a dry run, AFTER the (2) gate so a refused module is never announced as something that would have happened. `lib/bootstrap.sh` also converts its previously **silent** drop of a profile-named high-risk module into an `io_alert` naming it — a profile that lists `dns` used to just quietly not run it. **(4) Considered, measured, and REJECTED — a stronger consent gate, recorded rather than silently dropped:** refusing a high-risk selection whenever `FS_YES != 1` and stdin is not a TTY (a piped `y` satisfying the opt-in prompt). It was implemented and it broke `tests/fixtures/install.sh` (4 FAILs: the two high-risk opt-in cells plus the `profile: selection` pin) because those reviewed cells drive opt-in + confirm ENTIRELY by piped stdin — refusing every piped high-risk selection makes the whole P4.7 consent flow untestable without a pty. It also contradicts this task's own wording, which names a *direct `install dns` invocation* as consent, and it defends against nothing: a script able to pipe `y` into `./setup` can just run `nmcli`. Reverted; the fixture now pins the contract that actually exists (naming the module is consent, the confirmation is real, declining it aborts rc1 before any batch) and the header records why the stronger gate was rejected so it is not re-proposed blind. `tests/fixtures/risk_gate.sh` (new, **56 asserts**): the color claim statically (the `-z` gate must be gone, the numeric compare must be present in both files) AND rendered through a **pty** allocated by a new `fx_pty_available` / `fx_pty_run` helper in `tests/fixtures/lib.sh` — the red `[31m` must reach a pty stdout, `FS_NO_COLOR=1` on a pty must emit none, and piped stdout must stay free of ANSI. A python3-backed pty is a NEW test dependency, so the color cells degrade to a labelled `SKIP` when python3 is absent rather than failing a portable host. Profile exclusion is checked DYNAMICALLY — every module in the tree is classified and every shipped profile cross-checked, so a future P8 module cannot drift into a profile unnoticed, and each high-risk module must also declare `MODULE_DEFAULT=off`; plus a LIVE dep injection (`MODULE_DEPENDS="locale"` appended to a copy of `core`) proving the (2) gate refuses rc1 with `localectl` never reached; a profile that genuinely names `locale` asserting it warns and still runs its safe modules rc0; a dry-run alert per high-risk module plus the negative case that a safe module (`core`) gets none; the consent cells; and `io_alert`'s own contract (stdout exactly `!! alert body`, stderr empty, audit-logged, bold on a pty). **Mutation-checked: every one of the four guards is load-bearing** — restoring the `-z` gate → 4 FAILs, removing the runner transitive gate → 3 FAILs (rc 0 and `# would run: sudo localectl` reappear), removing the dry-run alert → 4 FAILs; the rejected (4) gate was mutation-checked the same way (8 FAILs) before being reverted. Evidence: `bash -n` clean on every shell file; risk_gate **56/0** rc0; smoke **125/0** rc0; **38 fixture suites, 2747 asserts, 0 failed, all rc0** WITH the `/tmp/opencode/ubin` rpm shim on PATH (up from 36 suites / 2691 — the two new suites are `risk_gate.sh`; `install.sh` and `install_profiles.sh` still green, which is the P4.7 no-regression proof); the full battery byte-identical across two consecutive runs; `git diff --check` clean; modes 644. **Real host, read-only and UNCHANGED:** `/etc/locale.conf` md5 still `164aba1ef1298affaa58761647f2ceba`, `localectl status` still `System Locale: LANG=en_US.UTF-8` — no live mutation was performed in this task. Bash 4.3 floor: **not run** (no podman/docker on this host; `unshare -Ur` sandbox-blocked) — static audit only: the new `curated`/`risks` associative arrays and the `pulled` array are declared with `local -A`/`local -a` (Bash 4.0+) and every expansion is `$#`-guarded; `fx_pty_run` uses only `pty.fork`/`execvp`, no namerefs. Carried: **no Senior Review (see the top of this row)**; the new pty helper depends on python3 and is the only test in the repo that does (SKIP, not FAIL, when absent); `io_alert` is deliberately stdout+un-timestamped, which is a departure from the timestamped `io_*` convention and is justified in its header; and the (4) decision is an interpretation of the task's ambiguous "explicit `--yes` or a direct invocation" wording, so it is **flagged for the owner at the P8 report** rather than assumed settled. |

| P9.4 Self-update + explicit pull (`./setup update`) | **DONE** · **COMMITTED** `595ab4a` | rev `ses_f0e3e7018ffe4jCCzJ8PPkjGY6` (R1 **PASS** 0 blocking + 13 NB → R2 **REVISE** 1 blocking (the collision guard) + 7 NB → R3 **REVISE** 1 blocking (two more fail-open predicates in that same guard) + 9 NB → R4 **PASS**, 0 blocking → R5 **PASS**, 0 blocking, re-confirming the tree after two taken recommendations). `lib/update.sh` REPORTS by default; only `update --pull` moves the working tree, replacing the deleted prototype's silent auto-pull. Every merge is `git merge --ff-only "$up"`, so this tool can never create a merge commit or rewrite local history; divergence is reported as its own state and refused with advice, never "resolved". The root comes from `$0` and **every** git call carries `-C "$root"`, so `./setup update` run from inside another checkout inspects THIS repository. **The guard that matters, and three review rounds of real data-loss holes in it:** git refuses to fast-forward over an untracked-but-NOT-ignored file, but silently **REPLACES an untracked-and-IGNORED** one — and `status --porcelain` reports nothing for it, so the P9.4 cleanliness gate was blind to exactly the case `.gitignore` creates (this repo ignores `downloads/`, `assets/*`, `.state/`). `--ff-only` does not help (the fast-forward is legitimate) and the HEAD==upstream postcondition is *satisfied* (HEAD did move), so the tool reported `updated to <sha>`, rc 0, with the user's file destroyed. **R2** is that hole. **R3** found two more predicates in the replacement guard failing OPEN, both reproduced end to end through `./setup update --pull` at rc 0: `[[ -e ]]` is **false for a dangling symlink** (a symlink into an unmounted drive — exactly what lives under `assets/wallpaper/`), and `git ls-files --error-unmatch -- "$path"` treats the path as a **glob pathspec**, so an incoming `pkg1?2.deb` is answered by a tracked `pkg1x2.deb` and the guard concludes there is nothing to lose. The shipped rule: refuse when the fast-forward would ADD a path that **exists on disk (a `-e` OR a `-L`) and is untracked**, the untracked test asked with `--literal-pathspecs` in git's **global** position (after the subcommand git rejects it with rc 129), and the on-disk test ordered first so `ls-files` spawns only for a local collision. It is deliberately NOT `status --ignored` (would refuse on this repo's own artifacts forever) nor `check-ignore` on incoming paths (would refuse an incoming file at an ignored path when nothing local is in the way) — both rejections were re-measured by the reviewer, not assumed. **A finding with no home in the tool:** `git check-ignore` is itself a pathspec command, so `check-ignore -q 'pkg1?2.deb'` reports "not ignored" when a tracked `pkg1x2.deb` exists; it accepts neither `:(literal)` nor `--literal-pathspecs` ("pathspec magic not supported by this command"), so `--no-index` is the only correct form, and the fixture's own precondition lied until it was rewritten. Also established: `[[ -e ]]` is not "exists on disk" (it follows symlinks), `--ff-only` needs a postcondition (`HEAD == "$up"`, not the merge's rc), a dry run that cannot fetch must render the fetch, state plainly that the comparison was NOT performed, and never guess from a stale `@{u}`; the rendered merge carries a literal `RESOLVED-AT-RUN-TIME` rather than a plausible-looking ref. `FS_PULL` is **not** an env seam — `cli_parse` resets it unconditionally like `FS_MANIFEST`, so an inherited `FS_PULL=1` cannot reintroduce the auto-pull — and `--pull` is refused on every command but `update`. Report-only mode does fetch, so it writes git objects, `FETCH_HEAD` and remote-tracking refs while leaving the working tree alone; that is documented rather than glossed. **Verification.** `tests/fixtures/update.sh` (new, **141 asserts**) drives the real launcher against **real git repositories** (one bare origin, seeded, then cloned per cell). Cells: version/identity, all four compare states (up-to-date / behind / ahead / diverged) plus detached HEAD and no-upstream, clean-pull, refusal on dirty (staged, unstaged, untracked), not-a-repo, no-git, cwd-never-decides (run under `env -i`), dry-run honesty, **a dry run must invoke git ZERO times** (a recording shim, and a report *does* fetch), the three collision cells (12c ignored-untracked, **12d dangling symlink**, **12e glob pathspec**) each asserting its preconditions first, **12f the false-refusal direction** — a tracked file the fast-forward modifies is not a collision — with four *state* oracles rather than a message, the direct-call bogus-`FS_PULL` cell proving the pull gate fails CLOSED, stray arguments, help text, a leading-zero count that is not octal, a lying git, an unreachable remote, and no state root created. **Four defect classes the review forced, all recorded in AGENTS §12:** a path handed to git is a PATHSPEC (`--` ends options, not pathspec magic); `[[ -e ]]` is not existence; a merge needs a postcondition like a package install does; and a dry run that cannot reach a seam must say so instead of faking it. **Evidence:** `bash -n` clean on all changed/new shell files; `bash tests/smoke.sh` **169/0** rc0; **42/42 fixture suites** rc0, 0 `FAIL` lines, all WITH the `/tmp/opencode/ubin` rpm shim on PATH (the pre-existing no-rpm host gap, NOT a P9.4 result); `tests/fixtures/update.sh` **141/0**; three full runs of `bash tests/smoke.sh` followed by every `tests/fixtures/*.sh` in sorted order byte-identical (`01ee89c36cdc769f`, timestamp and fixture temp-dir name normalized — comparable only like-for-like); **24 mutations, all caught**, each on a throwaway copy and each `cmp`-checked non-vacuous first, including the reviewer's own sets which found mutations the implementer's had missed. The three guard mutations: `-L` removed → 6 FAILs, `--literal-pathspecs` removed → 5, tracked-file test removed → 5. `--diff-filter` carries `C` as well as `AMR`; the copy case is covered by inspection only, since the copy constructible under `diff.renames=copies` classifies as an add. One limit is left standing and documented in the header: an upstream path containing a literal newline is not inspected, because the incoming list is read through `$(...)`, which cannot carry NUL. **Known verification gap (P9.4) — NOT met, do not claim it:** the task is tagged `[MOCK]`, and that is what was delivered: no cell fast-forwards the user's actual checkout and no `[REAL]` criterion was attempted. A fast-forward was exercised end to end against real git objects in throwaway clones, which is a mechanics result, not a result about someone's working tree. The R2/R3 defects were found by **reviewer measurement of git's own behaviour against scenarios the suite did not cover at all** — no mutation exposed them, because the cells did not exist — which is the argument for auditing a destructive guard's coverage directly, and a reminder that the "no data loss" claim is only as good as the ignore rules a future change introduces. |
| P9.1 Preflight check (`./setup check`) | **DONE** · **COMMITTED** `21f919a` | OWNER-APPROVED at the P9.1 gate (no fresh-context Senior Review — deliberate, at the owner's explicit instruction, and the FIRST such deviation after the owner restored the mandatory review loop for P9.2 onward; this row therefore records **no `ses_…` id and no R1/R2 rounds**, and the absence is a process fact, not a claim of review). **Scope delivered, exactly the §4 What:** distro known, network reachability (flathub + GitHub), pkgmgr present, non-root + sudo usable, flatpak present, disk-space note — and an exit code that reflects WARN vs FAIL. `lib/check.sh` (new) + `lib/bootstrap.sh` `_cli_check_impl` replacing the `_cli_check_stub` (deleted, not left dead) + `tests/fixtures/check.sh` (new) + the `tests/smoke.sh` cell de-stubbed. **Exit code, one rule with one source of truth:** the worst level found decides — any FAIL -> rc1, WARNs included -> rc0. The verdict is the LAST LINE of the same `io_summary` block that holds the rows, so the table and the exit code are derived from the same variable and cannot disagree; the fixture re-derives that invariant independently on all 24 cells. A warning means "degraded but not blocking", so folding WARN into a non-zero rc would make the word a lie: an **offline host is rc0** (both reachability rows are WARN) while a **missing sudo** and an **unknown distro** are rc1 — which is precisely the §4 Verification matrix. **Read-only contract, and two real defects the fixture caught in the developer's own work** (both the AGENTS §12 "plausible condition that silently never fires" class, and both now pinned): (1) `state_init` was called unconditionally, so a preflight on a host that had never installed anything **created** `~/.local/state/fedora-setup` and the "no state dir yet" row was dead code — it is now called ONLY when the state root already exists, so a fresh HOME keeps having none (the P4.6 registry is still read via `state_module_check` when it does exist). (2) `_check_state_root` printed its path and the caller captured it with `$(...)`, so the no-state-base FAIL was raised **inside a subshell**: `_CHECK_WORST` was updated on a copy and the message was swallowed into the captured value, and a host with no `HOME`/`XDG_STATE_HOME`/`FS_HOME` reported `PASS modules: no state dir yet` and **rc0** — proven live before the fix. It now sets a global and FAILs rc1. **Design decisions:** network reachability deliberately gets **no seam** — the probe client is chosen from PATH (curl, then wget), so the offline / online / no-client arms are driven by PATH alone and the fixture needs no special-casing to reach them; added seams are `FS_CHECK_MIN_FREE_MB` (default 2048) and `FS_CHECK_NET_TIMEOUT` (default 5), both malformed-value-safe. `FS_DISTRO_FAMILY` is deliberately NOT honored — reporting what was actually detected is this command's entire job, so detection always runs. `FS_PKG_BACKEND=mock` skips the pkgmgr row with a WARN (so the all-PASS baseline cell runs with a real fake `dnf5` and a non-mock backend instead, or it could never be all-PASS). Dry-run suppresses the only two probes that leave the machine (network + sudo policy) and reports those rows as "skipped (dry run probes nothing)" rather than guessing; local free probes (`command -v`, `df`, reading os-release) still run. The privilege check reuses `sudo_detect`'s canonical policy globals and adds only a `command -v sudo` split so "needs a password" can be a WARN instead of being flattened into the same answer as "no sudo at all"; the only probe is sudo's own `-n true`, which cannot prompt and cannot hang. **`tests/fixtures/check.sh` (new, 120 asserts, 24 cells)** driving the REAL launcher: a hermetic PATH "farm" of REGULAR-FILE binaries, **rebuilt per cell** so no cell inherits another's removals; `guard_fake` asserts every fake is isolated and has a self-test proving it rejects a leaky farm — the P8.1 symlink-leak lesson applied PREEMPTIVELY, because a leaked real `sudo`/`curl` would turn a FAIL cell into a silent PASS, and because `FS_EUID=0` removes the sudo barrier. Cells: all-PASS baseline; offline (fake curl exits 7) -> WARN + rc0; missing sudo (PATH genuinely has none) -> FAIL + rc1; sudo needs a password -> WARN + rc0; passwordless sudo -> PASS; the sudo probe NEVER escalates (only `-n true` is ever logged); unknown distro / os-release with no ID / unreadable os-release -> FAIL + rc1; `FS_DISTRO_FAMILY` does NOT short-circuit detection; absent pkgmgr -> FAIL; real pkgmgr -> PASS; mock backend -> WARN skip; flatpak present -> PASS / absent -> WARN; no HTTP client -> WARN with NO probe attempted; the wget arm; disk PASS and WARN; malformed seams fall back to defaults; no `df` -> WARN, never a guess; dry run probes nothing (the fake client is never called); a fresh HOME gains NO state root; the P4.6 registry is read (2 marked modules); an unusable state root -> FAIL (the state.sh confinement guard, reached via a symlinked root, which is what makes the existence gate and the guard both load-bearing); NO resolvable state base -> FAIL (the subshell regression); missing modules dir -> WARN; the summary is audit-logged. **Evidence:** `bash -n` clean on all 4 changed/new files; smoke **127 passed, 0 failed** rc0; **38 fixture suites, 0 failing**, all rc0, WITH the `/tmp/opencode/ubin` rpm shim on PATH (the pre-existing no-rpm host gap still makes `planner.sh` need that shim — P6.4-ticketed, NOT a P9.1 result); `check.sh` **120/0** rc0 x3 **byte-identical**; smoke byte-identical x2. **`[REAL]`: read-only only, and no mutation was performed or is claimed.** On this host `./setup check` reports fedora/rpm/dnf5, non-root with passwordless sudo unavailable -> WARN, both hosts reachable, 34164 MB free, and the 6 real marked modules (core flatpak fonts git gnome-extensions terminal), rc0; a `find -newermt` sweep over the state tree across the whole session proves the run wrote **0 files**. No `podman`/`docker` on this host and `unshare -Ur` fails with `write failed /proc/self/uid_map: Operation not permitted`, so the bash-4.3 floor is a static audit only: no namerefs/`mapfile`/`${var,,}`/`local -A`/unguarded `"${arr[@]}"`; every array expansion is `$#`- or `${arr[@]+…}`-guarded. Carried: the `state_init` call still creates its own four subdirectories when the state root already exists — that is `state.sh`'s own behaviour inside a directory the user already has, and the header states it rather than claiming zero creation; the new seams are not yet in AGENTS §3 (phase-boundary refresh, as with P7.1–P7.3); `FS_MODULES_DIR` is honored for the modules row, so a caller can point the row at a different tree. |
| P9.2 Verify audit (`./setup verify`) | **DONE** · **COMMITTED** `902b528` | rev `ses_f1078edc3ffeNv3zb1H8sweZQ6` (R1 **REVISE** B1/B2/B3 + 7 NB → R2 **REVISE** B4 (ordering) + 7 NB → R3 **PASS**, 0 blocking). Read-only by construction: installs nothing, marks nothing, backs nothing up, never sudoes, and never creates a state root. `lib/status.sh` is **extracted from `lib/check.sh`** (not duplicated) so `check` and `verify` share one PASS/WARN/FAIL table and one exit-code rule — any FAIL -> rc1, WARNs only -> rc0, with the verdict derived from the same variable as the rows so the table and the exit code cannot disagree. `lib/verify.sh` selects its work set by explicit ids -> `--profile` closure -> the P4.6 module registry -> all modules, then runs the generic layer (system packages through the P3.9 `pkg_verify_packages`, flatpaks through the flatpak backend, the P7.5 alt id deduped against the module's own list so the alternative is never double-counted) plus a per-module `verify()` hook. **The hook rc protocol, and the finding that forced it:** a hook's rc is a VERDICT, so `lib/verify.sh` owns the two "not applicable" signals (`_VERIFY_HOOK_NONE=90` no hook, `_VERIFY_HOOK_UNDEFINED=91` `hooks.sh` without a `verify()`) and renders **every other value** as a hook verdict. An earlier revision used rc 2/3 for its own two signals, so a `verify()` returning 2 to mean FAILURE was reported as a WARN "module has no verify() hook" with process rc 0 — a real failure audited clean, with the module's whole point inverted. No shipped hook may return 90/91; even a hook that does fails LOUD, never silent. Three modules gain `verify()` hooks, closing the ROADMAP's "fonts" and "managed files" categories that no shipped module covered: `git` (managed block + section headers), `fonts` (per-set marker, 3 entries), `terminal` (aliases block, managed blocks, OMP theme). `lib/state.sh` gains `state_root_path` — a pure reader resolving the root WITHOUT creating it, so a read-only command can report "no state dir yet" instead of manufacturing one. **Four false-PASS defects across three review rounds, all the same shape — checking a weaker thing than the thing claimed:** **B1** `git` checked the four config *values* but not the section headers, so a block git reads as entirely inert (a setting outside any section is ignored) audited clean; **B2** `terminal` asserted one of the five aliases it installs; **B3** `fonts` dropped `list_parse`'s exit status inside a herestring, so an unreadable config yielded zero entries and reported "passed (0 sets installed)" while `run()` failed on the same file — the AGENTS §12 rule that a command substitution in a herestring does not propagate the substituted status; **B4** the ordered-relation defect: after all three hooks were switched to the **shared body-builder** their own `run()` uses (`_git_body`, `_term_aliases_body`/`_term_block`, `_font_marker`), a fully green suite still reported a **reordered** managed block as clean, because the auditor compared line **sets** while `fs_managed_block` — the writer — compares line **arrays** (`_fs_array_equal`: length first, then element-wise ordered). Every line was present and correct, so the set matched; the file was not what `run()` writes, so the writer would rewrite it, and since `git` is section-scoped the swapped pair had put both values in the wrong section and git read neither. The rule recorded in AGENTS §12: **an oracle derived from the installer closes shared-assumption drift, but it does NOT close a defect in how the installer and the auditor COMPARE that oracle — the auditor's comparison relation must BE the writer's relation, not merely be built from the writer's data.** Both hooks now compare ordered and length-sensitive over identically normalized lines (leading whitespace stripped, blank lines dropped), and that normalization is deliberately **strictly permissive**: it can only turn a mismatch into a match, never a false FAIL, so the slack sits in the safe direction (the reviewer probed 16 round-trip variants through the real writer and real verifier for false FAILs and found zero). The per-line set reporting is kept but demoted to **diagnostic-only**. `tests/fixtures/verify.sh` (new, **272 asserts**) drives the real launcher and derives every known-good state from the hooks' own body-builders, never hand-written: selection cells (explicit ids / `--profile` closure / registry / all), every verdict level and the aggregate exit code, the 90/91 reserved-signal cells, a hook returning 2/3 rendered as FAIL, dry-run renders a framework-generated skipped WARN and executes no hook, read-only proofs (no install/remove/backup/mark, no sudo, no state root created), stale-registry continuation, and the three module hooks with their mutations — headerless block, all five aliases, unreadable config, missing font dir, missing OMP theme, absent posh/atuin binaries (informational, not fatal), **reordered block, duplicated line, two adjacent blocks, two overlapping blocks**, and a reorder-mutation self-check asserting the mutation is not a no-op. That last guard exists because the first reorder cell in this round silently changed nothing and would have passed vacuously — the same lesson applied to my own test. Evidence: `bash -n` clean on all 10 changed/new shell files; verify **272/0** rc0 with stdout md5 `f99d6a4bf1c3c9a30598927deba959de` **byte-identical x3**; smoke **138/0** rc0; **39 fixture suites, 0 failing**, all rc0, WITH the `/tmp/opencode/ubin` rpm shim on PATH (the pre-existing no-rpm host gap still makes `planner.sh` need that shim — P6.4-ticketed, NOT a P9.2 result); `mod_git.sh`/`mod_fonts.sh`/`mod_terminal.sh` green, which is the proof the shared-body-builder refactor did not change what `run()` writes. **Known verification gap (P9.2) — NOT met, do not claim it:** the `[REAL]`/live-host half is **DEFERRED**. No `podman`/`docker` on this host and `unshare -Ur` fails with `write failed /proc/self/uid_map: Operation not permitted`, so **no containerized Bash-4.3 floor run and no real-distro audit were performed** — the floor is a **static audit only** (no namerefs/`mapfile`/`${var,,}`/unguarded `"${arr[@]}"`; every array expansion `$#`- or `${arr[@]+...}`-guarded). A live-host **read-only** smoke was run and did surface real drift, but it is supplemental, not the criterion. The sharper limit, also recorded in AGENTS §1: a fully green fixture proves the audit and its oracle agree, **NOT** that the audit agrees with the system — which is exactly how three false-PASS defects survived a 215-assert suite, and why the oracle is now derived from the hooks' own body-builders. Carried: the ROADMAP §4 "What" also lists *repo files, gsettings values, extensions* as verify categories — **not implemented in P9.2**; they belong to the P6 gnome hooks and the P7.5 `vscode` prerepo surface and are deliberately not duplicated here, so P9.3/P9.4 and the P6 closure decide their fate. The read-only guarantee is documented honestly as "no install/remove, no state mark/backup, no sudo" rather than "touches nothing": `state_init` creates its standard subdirectories when the state root already exists (the P9.1 behaviour), and a vendor CLI may write its own cache. The auditor is one place **stricter** than the writer — an empty managed block is a FAIL where `fs_managed_block` would accept it — which is unreachable for any shipped body because the shared builders are structural; recorded, not changed. New `verify()` seams are not yet listed in AGENTS §3 (phase-boundary refresh, as with P7.1–P7.3 / P9.1). 
| P9.3 Export snapshot + `--manifest` re-import (`./setup export`) | **DONE** · **COMMITTED** `69ae219` | rev `ses_f0eb1e4beffespAPKxmBn5HCwJ` (R1 **REVISE** 5 blocking + 14 NB → R2 **REVISE** 1 blocking (the completion marker) + 4 NB → R3 **REVISE** 1 blocking (the marker, one stage too late) + 4 NB + N14 undecided → R4 **PASS**, 0 blocking). `lib/export.sh` writes a re-importable, timestamp-free snapshot and `install --manifest <dir>` replays it through the **ordinary** runner, so the plan installed is the plan exported rather than the live one. Scope is allowlist-by-construction: the resolved `<profile>.conf`, per-module `package`/`flatpak` manifests, an allowlisted gsettings key set, **enabled** extensions, installed font markers, and managed blocks only. Every file is always written (empty when there is nothing to record) so the tree shape is host-independent, and the exporter reads **no part of the state root**. `state/` and `files/` are a review/diff record only — nothing replays them; `verify` does not read an export tree either. `FS_MANIFEST`/`FS_PROFILE_SET` are **deliberately not env seams**: both are set only by their flags and `cli_parse` resets them unconditionally, so an inherited environment value can never redirect a run into an untrusted tree. **Four defects the review found, all the same shape — the artifact looked right while a *different* artifact governed behaviour:** **B1** (R2) `export.meta` was written FIRST, so a failed export left a directory a re-import **accepted** and silently half-populated; it is now the **completion marker** and is written last. **B2** (R3) that was still not enough: re-export over an existing tree is allowed, and the OLD marker survived a refresh that failed inside the **manifest loop**, so the tree replayed the *previous* profile over the *new* ids — a combination no export ever produced, with nothing reporting an error anywhere. The marker is now dropped immediately after the outdir preflight and before the loop (the first stage that writes), so the window in which a partial tree is still recognizable is empty; it is deliberately **not** dropped during argument/profile validation, which writes nothing, so a run rejected for a bad `--profile` leaves a previous good tree valid. **B3** the absent/no-manifest fix was pinned for the *package* namespace only, so the flatpak half was untested. **B4** `lib/modules.sh`'s family gate was not manifest-aware, which made a real re-import path (a deb tree replayed onto an rpm host) impossible. The rule recorded in AGENTS §12: **a completion marker only works if it is invalidated for the whole window in which the tree is being rewritten, not merely re-added at the end.** **The block reader shares the writer's code.** `files/<name>` is located with `lib/fs.sh`'s own `_fs_validate_markers`/`_fs_locate`/`_fs_locate_ok` rather than a parser in `export.sh`, because `fs_managed_block` owns the meaning of "the managed block"; a private parser concatenated the bodies of a **duplicated** block into one the writer **refuses** to manage. After the shared validator passes, `_fs_locate_ok` can only fail on the duplicate case, so a separate "unterminated" check is **unreachable** — a mutation removing it produced 0 FAILs, which is how the dead branch was found and deleted. Absence (no marker) and truncation (marker with no END) are kept distinct: conflating them made every block-less dotfile warn and then contradict itself with "no managed block". **Verification.** `tests/fixtures/export.sh` (new, **184 asserts**) drives the real launcher and copies the module tree privately, never mutating the repo. Cells: tree shape + content, secrets structurally excluded, managed-block-only extraction, real font markers, determinism, GNOME content (allowlist boundary, enabled-only extensions, tab-bearing value skipped), the round trip, **manifest authority via drift** (the live list changes, the manifest install does not), a module absent from the manifest, every refusal, the recorded profile, dry-run purity, read-only proof, empty profile, symlinked managed file, the flag itself. **The ROADMAP criterion is "the re-import dry-runs identically", which is NOT a sufficient test on its own** — it is satisfied by any implementation that quietly re-reads the live tree. The equivalence cell and the drift/authority cell are therefore one criterion; either alone is satisfiable by the wrong code (that was the round-1 B3 finding). Three **refresh** cells pin the completion marker from three different failure points — a late stage (`_export_fonts`), inside the manifest loop (module 2 invalid, asserting module 1's manifest was already rewritten so the cell cannot pass by the refresh never starting), and the **last** stage (`_export_files`, the edge the reviewer found unpinned as `g12`). Evidence: `bash -n` clean on all 7 changed/new scripts; `bash tests/smoke.sh` **161/0** rc0; **41/41 fixture suites** rc0, all WITH the `/tmp/opencode/ubin` rpm shim on PATH (the pre-existing no-rpm host gap, NOT a P9.3 result); `tests/fixtures/export.sh` **184/0**; three full runs byte-identical (`a207b401c51600523abd7e06a347c136`); every mutation checked for non-vacuity with `cmp` first, so a no-op mutation cannot masquerade as a passing cell. **Process note, recorded honestly:** the reviewer's R3 mutation battery leaked into the **live** tree (its `m0` snapshot was taken while already mutated, so its restores were incomplete) and reverted three files mid-review; this was detected by the reviewer, restored from backups, and re-verified file by file. Two of my own `s.replace()` edits had no assert and **silently no-op'd**, which is why the block-extraction header documentation was missing from `lib/export.sh` at R4 and had to be rewritten — the same "plausible-looking code whose failure path was never observed" class as P8.3, applied to my own tooling. **Known verification gap (P9.3) — NOT met, do not claim it:** the `[REAL]`/live-host half is **DEFERRED**; no `podman`/`docker` on this host and `unshare -Ur` fails with `write failed /proc/self/uid_map: Operation not permitted`. The tree was exercised hermetically against the mock backend and read-only on this host, but **nothing here proves an export matches a real system's installed state**, and a byte-identical re-export is a determinism result, not a correctness one. Format documented in `docs/export-format.md`. Carried: new `export` seams are listed in AGENTS §3; `FS_MANIFEST`/`FS_PROFILE_SET` are reworded there as variables, not env seams (reviewer's N14 call (a), code untouched).

Fixed with `set +e` in `setup()` AND by making `t_rc` itself errexit-safe (`|| rc=$?`). **Two blocking findings, both on the failure path (exactly when a CI parser must read the output):** B1 the awk renumbering treated `not ok` as `$1="not"`, so a failing bats test emitted `not 12 not ok 12 name` — invalid TAP and desynced every later number (the implementer's failure-path evidence only exercised a *fixture* failure, which never goes through bats' `not ok`, which is why it escaped); B2 fixture FAIL lines used a 4-space indent, which is not a valid TAP line. The P10.3 criterion 'CI pipeline green across the matrix' is therefore UNVERIFIED; what is verified is (i) the YAML parses and the matrix enumerates four entries, (ii) the script passes locally under Podman as root against real backends on real distro images, and (iii) the podman invocation the workflow uses was exercised by hand -- including the two defects above, which is how they were caught before any CI run existed. The `[REAL]`/live-host half of the criterion is met only in the 'local container execution' sense, never in the 'CI' sense. The ROADMAP §5 wording for P10 is 'CI green: lint + unit + container matrix', so this phase is NOT fully closed until a real Actions run exists. **THREE LIMITATIONS THE INDEPENDENT REVIEW FOUND WERE NOT DISCLOSED AT ALL when this row was first written. They are recorded here rather than left implicit.** **NB-1 (the most serious finding of the review, and NOT a code defect but a coverage limit) -- the `[REAL]` assertions are the tool's opinion of itself; there is no independent installed-state postcondition.** `smoke.sh`'s real-install asserts (`run complete`, `2 modules ok`, `artifacts in`) are all rendered by `lib/summary.sh` from the runner's OWN return codes, and nothing ever asks the package manager whether the packages are installed. This is AGENTS §12's 'a green fixture proves only that the audit and its oracle agree' class, and the reviewer demonstrated it is not hypothetical from this repo's OWN cited evidence: `/tmp/p103real5.log:1762-1768` (the debian transcript cited above) shows three `bwrap: Creating new namespace failed: Operation not permitted` lines immediately followed by `2 modules ok · 0 skipped · 0 failed` and rc 0 -- the tool reported success right after three flatpak sandbox errors and the suite called that a pass. `lib/verify.sh` (P9.2) already implements the independent read-only oracle (`pkg_verify_packages` + flatpak verification, never sudoes), and this container suite is the ONLY place in the repo where `./setup verify` could run against a genuinely real installed system. **OWNER DECISION, NOT TAKEN IN THIS PHASE** (it would extend the task's scope, and `ROADMAP.md:879` scopes the real install as 'where possible'): adding `./setup verify --profile minimal` after the first real install would give the `[REAL]` half an independent oracle. Recorded as an open recommendation, explicitly NOT done. **NB-3 -- the dry-run fingerprint watches only four trees, so it cannot see a real system-package write.** `smoke.sh`'s `fingerprint()` covers `$STATE_ROOT`, `$HOME`, `/etc` and `$ROOT`. A dry-run regression that actually EXECUTED a system batch would write `/var/lib/dpkg`, `/var/lib/rpm`, `/var/cache/flatpak` or `/var/lib/flatpak` -- outside the watched set -- and every assert would stay green. Flatpak writes land under `$HOME/.local/share/flatpak` and ARE covered. The header enumerates the four trees honestly and `run_cmd`'s dry-run mechanism is covered hermetically, so this is a disclosed SAMPLE, not an overclaim; the independent reviewer nonetheless confirmed the fingerprint is load-bearing by its own mutation (`runner.sh:328` `FS_DRY_RUN != 1` -> `if ((1))`: 17/0 -> 15/2, both the fingerprint and the state-absent assert fire). Tension worth naming: the suite certifies a `# would run:` render that is KNOWN WRONG in the exact environment it runs in (root; see the sudo defect below), so the fingerprint and the render are the two places where NB-3 and the sudo defect compound. **NB-7 -- no assert-count pin, and two guards silently drop asserts.** The `ctr_id != "unknown"` guard and the `FS_CTR_FAMILY`-set guard each skip one assert with no counter, so a green job with fewer checks is invisible. Neither is reachable as CI is configured (the workflow hard-fails an unhandled ID and always passes the family), so this is a robustness note rather than a live false green. **NB-8 -- two citation line-ranges are off by one** (`scripts/lint:37-40` should be 36-41; `lib/runner.sh:114-119` should be 115-120). Cosmetic. **NB-9 -- `fe70695` commits `ROADMAP.md`, which AGENTS §10 and `.gitignore:58` say must never be committed.** Pre-existing and NOT a P10.3 defect: the file has been tracked since `5ab2d83`, the ignore rule was added LATER at `4457172` and has therefore never had any effect, and 19 prior commits touch it. The permanent contradiction between §10 and repo state is a documentation decision, not a code fix, and is left as found. **Also NOT verified, stated rather than implied:** the root dry-run sudo-rendering defect at `lib/bootstrap.sh:399-402` (dry-run skips `sudo_detect`, so a root dry run renders `sudo apt-get`/`sudo pacman` while a real root run would not). It is PRE-EXISTING and out of P10.3 scope, and the smoke script deliberately does NOT assert on sudo prefixes, so this suite neither hides nor institutionalises it. A Bash 4.3 floor run was not performed. GNOME coverage is absent by design (headless container: no session bus, no display, no gsettings schema) and is skipped with a printed reason, never claimed. |




## P0 — Planning & Repository Baseline

### 1. Phase objective
Produce this roadmap, capture an identical snapshot of the working prototype, and record the
migration boundaries before any file is touched.

### 2. Why this phase exists
The current code is the only working reference. Every subsequent decision (what to preserve,
what to delete, how to verify behavior) must be traceable to a frozen baseline. Without a
baseline, P1's destructive deletions are unrecoverable-by-humans.

### 3. Prerequisites / dependencies
- None (fresh start).

### 4. Ordered tasks

- **P0.1** `[SEQ]` Create this `ROADMAP.md` and add it to `.gitignore`. **[DONE]**
  - **What:** Author the roadmap; add a `ROADMAP.md` ignore entry.
  - **Verification:** `git check-ignore ROADMAP.md` returns the path; `git status --short` shows nothing for it.
  - **Depends:** —

- **P0.2** `[SEQ]` Tag the prototype baseline. **[DONE]**
  - **What:** `git tag proto-baseline` (lightweight tag at `HEAD` `048f969`).
  - **Verification:** `git tag` lists `proto-baseline`; working tree still clean.
  - **Depends:** P0.1

- **P0.3** `[SEQ]` Record baseline inventory. **[DONE]**
  - **What:** Document current file tree, per-script behavior summaries, and the feature→module mapping table
    (feature, old script, new module id, keep/rewrite/drop). Store in `docs/` later (P11); keep notes here in the
    interim as `BASELINE_NOTES` section at the end of this file.
  - **Verification:** Feature mapping table is complete: every old script has an explicit disposition.
  - **Depends:** P0.2

- **P0.4** `[SEQ]` Define migration boundaries. **[DONE]**
  - **What:** Decide explicitly what P1 deletes wholesale vs. preserves.
    - DELETE (git-recoverable): `scripts/*.sh`, `additions/fonts/**`, `additions/shell_conf`, stale README claims.
    - PRESERVE as user assets (optional, gitignored content): wallpaper, icons.
    - PRESERVE as concepts: every feature becomes a module (see feature mapping).
  - **Verification:** Boundary list is written down in this file; no ambiguities remain.
  - **Depends:** P0.3

### 5. Verification criteria
- `git tag proto-baseline` exists; working tree clean; ROADMAP is ignored; feature mapping complete.

### 6. Expected files / components affected
- `ROADMAP.md` (new, gitignored), `.gitignore` (1 line added), git tag.

### 7. Risks or blockers
- None. Pure planning.

### 8. Definition of Done
- Baseline frozen; boundaries written; roadmap approved to start P1.

---

## P1 — Repository Restructure & Bootstrap

### 1. Phase objective
Create the new directory skeleton, the `./setup` entry point, and the bootstrap layer; then
safely remove the obsolete prototype structure.

### 2. Why this phase exists
All later phases depend on a canonical layout and a single, CWD-independent entry point. Deleting
the old tree last (after a working skeleton exists) avoids ever leaving the repo with no runnable
artifact.

### 3. Prerequisites / dependencies
- P0 (all 4 tasks).
- Deletions in P1.3 are git-recoverable from `proto-baseline` (P0.2).

### 4. Ordered tasks

- **P1.1** `[SEQ]` Create the target directory skeleton.
  - **What:** Create empty dirs + `.gitkeep`/placeholder files:
    `lib/`, `modules/`, `profiles/`, `assets/{wallpaper,icons,fonts}/`, `docs/`, `tests/`.
  - **Verification:** `ls` shows the full tree; `git status` shows only new empty dirs.
  - **Depends:** P0.4

- **P1.2** `[SEQ]` Implement `./setup` entry point + `lib/bootstrap.sh`.
  - **What:** Executable `setup` that finds the repo root from `$0` (no CWD assumption), verifies Bash ≥ 4.3,
    required base tools (coreutils, `git` only for update, `curl`/`wget` for network modules), and forwards to
    the CLI dispatcher once it exists in P2. Until P2.8, bootstrap prints version + "CLI pending".
  - **Verification:** `./setup` and `bash setup` both work from the repo dir; `./setup` works from a
    *different* CWD via absolute/`$(dirname "$0")` path; bad interpreter or missing bash detected.
  - **Depends:** P1.1

- **P1.4** `[SEQ]` Harden `.gitignore`.
  - **What:** Track ignore rules for the state/log dir, mock/test scratch, and runtime downloads
    (e.g. `~/.local/state/fedora-setup` is outside repo, but any local caches inside repo must be ignored).
  - **Verification:** `git check-ignore` succeeds for each new pattern.
  - **Depends:** P1.1

- **P1.3** `[SEQ]` `[DESTROY]` Remove obsolete prototype files.
  - **What:** `git rm scripts/*.sh scripts/README.md additions/fonts/** additions/shell_conf`; rename/keep
    `additions/icons` + `additions/wallpaper.jpg` only if they survive as optional `assets/` (else delete too).
    Update old README references removed in P11.1 (leave README intact until docs phase to keep diff surface small —
    note: README is rewritten wholesale in P11.1).
  - **Verification:** `git status` shows the deletions staged; nothing referenced by the new tree is missing;
    `git checkout proto-baseline -- scripts/` restores everything (spot-check one file).
  - **Depends:** P1.2, P1.4 (skeleton must exist before deletion)

### 5. Verification criteria
- Full `./setup` entry works; old prototype gone but recoverable; ignore rules pass `git check-ignore`.

### 6. Expected files / components affected
- `setup`, `lib/bootstrap.sh`, `lib/` dir, `modules/`, `profiles/`, `assets/`, `docs/`, `tests/`, `.gitignore`,
  deleted `scripts/**`, `additions/fonts/**`.

### 7. Risks or blockers
- Deletion of working code before replacement is ready → mitigated by `proto-baseline` tag (P0.2).
- `setup` must not depend on CWD (users may clone anywhere).

### 8. Definition of Done
- Skeleton present; `./setup` runs; prototype deleted; git recoverable; tree clean.
